The Honorable
The Seoretary of State,
Washington.
Sir:
I have the honor to tranamit herewith for the Depart-
1/
ment's information a copy of a cartoon which appeared in
the evening edition of the Japen Times and Advertiser on
December 10, 1940, depicting Messrs. Matsuoka, Ribbentrop
and Ciano marching arm in arm and leading by the hand a
ohild which probably represents General Franco, the Spamish
diotator.

=== Page 2 === (confidence 0.98)
-2-
diotator. At the side of the cartoon there appears an
obnoxious effigy of President Roosevelt, with a sly
expression on his fece, holding out a money bag marked
with dollar signs and beckoning to the child. The
cartoon is labelled "At it again" but the President's
name is not mentioned.
Although I do not believe that anything is to be
gained by protesting to the Japanese Ministry of Foreign
Affairs I am sending the cartoon to the Department so
that it may be available should the Japanese Embassy in
Washington take occasion in the future to protest against
cartoons of the Emperor appearing in the American press.
Respectfully yours,
Joseph C. Grew

ties in the last European conflict.
."There is one point about the state
ment which is notable in a sense. If
contains a declaration to the world of
the intention on the part of Britain
and the United States to force the
Axis powers to restrict their arma-
ments, thereby perpetuating a world
order under which the destenies of
the nations of the world will be in the
hands of Britain and America who
alone will do the policing of the world.
It is easy to read between the lines of
the statement that Britain and America
intend to intimidate the Axis powers.
conci'iate the neutrals, and adopt a
threatening and at the same time con
ciliatory attitude toward Japan, alone
among all others.
Anglo-U.S. Scheme Exposed
"The leaders of Britain and America
are agreed in dividing the nations of
the world into two groups. One group
consists of nations bent on conques
and the other comprises peace-loving
nations. Their judgment is based on
superficial views.
"Presideni Roosevelt rejoices in up
holding the principle of lawfulness.
wish to believe that he does this ac
cording to the dictate of his consci-
ence.
"Now the principle of lawfulness is
a relic of the Versailles structure.
Under this principle the tyranny of
the surfeited states will be perpetuated
while the fate of all others states will
be emasculation, enfeeblement, and
self-destruction. The trend of world
events sinc? the last European war
has tended to strengthen the position
of the self-seeking countries having
the lion's share of the resources of the
world and bring the nations not favor-
ed by nature to the verge of ruin.
There is nothing remarkable about
this. since these world events have
largely been influenced by Britain and
America. The construction of large
economic blocs with their high tariff
walls, export permit systems, and im-
port quotas, which came after the
world war, was nothing more than the
death sentence pronounced on the Axis
powers.
"Heaven did not create superior

According to a recent letter received by a Japanese
in Argentina from his younger brother (names not given)
in Peru, Japanese in Peru have been persecuted in many
ways since the war started. Their assets have been fro-
zen, many heads of families have been forcibly shipped
to the United States where they were interned, and fam-
ilies broken up, and many of the wives and elders and
children remaining in Peru have siokened and died. Those
Japanese, as well as Germans and Italians remaining in
Peru have barely been able to eke out an existence, and
they can't even obtain treatment if they beoome ill. So
that it behooves us Japanese living in Argentina not to
become too habituated to our happy lot, and to tend to
our fields of work with serious diligence.

October 12, 1943: Beginning today the layout of the Diario Nan-A 1
being changed. The editorial, formerly on page 2 in Ja-
panese only, now appears on page 1 in both Japanese and
Spanish. Principal news items, formerly on page 1, now
appear on page 2, and page 1 1s completed with advertise-
ments. Pages 3 and 4 take any overflow of world news,
also local news, special features, want ads and notioes,
and general advertising. Both paper and print are poor-
est in quality of the 3 Japanese daily newspapers. Today's
editorial, the first under the new arrangement, sets forth
policy and is therefore translated in full as follows:
The Voice of "Diario Nan-A"
Starting today, we will publish in these pages, the
Spanish version of our editorials. This does not mean
that there has been any change in the stand or prinoiples
which this paper has been upholding đuring the eight years
since its foundation, and which are fully known to our
readers.
However, on commencing the publication of these Span-
ish editorials, we set out herewith an explanatory summary
of the prinoiples and the fundamental spirit adhered to in
the past which we will sustain firmly and invariably in
the future:
First: We Japanese residents abroad must uphold the
honor of our country, and must never commit acts to harm
1t. This means that we shall never laek loyalty towards
our country, and shall never forget our debt of gratitude

=== Page 62 === (confidence 0.99)
- 3 -
to the country of our birth and upbringing. (This being
the core of the matter, what follows may be considered
elaborate eyewash for the Argentine authorities.)
Second: We shall always remember that we are living
in the Argentine Republic, protected by its Constitution
and its laws, and enjoying a free and tranquil life; and
therefore we shall respect the Constitution and the laws
of this country which has welcomed us with generosity.
(The last phrase "which has welcomed us, etc." is omitted
fro the Japanese version.)
Third: We shall always be grateful to Argentina and
its people who, during the half-century we have resided
in the country, have always treated us with impartiality,
generosity and great sympathy. ("Great sympathy" is
"friendliness" in the Japanese version.)
Fourth: We must not only be grateful to the Argentine
Republic, but must also repay our great debt (repay Ar-
gentine kindness - in the Japanese version) by contribu-
ting to its development with our civic work and our labors.
Fifth: We shall endeavor to increase our knowledge of
the fundamental spirit of the founding of Argentina and
study its history, introduce these to Japan, and inorease
the knowledge and understanding of Japan by this great Re-
publio. ("by Argentina" in Japanese).
Sixth: We shall respect Argentine customs and aot
harmoniously with the Argentines.
Seventh: The education of our children born in Argen-
tina shall have as its object, the making of them into
good Argentine citizens, (in Japanese is added the expla-
nation: 1.e., make them love Argentina) so that they hall
be loyal to Argentina and (in Japanese is added: "toach
them Japanese, so that knowing Japan they will serve as
a bond of mutual comprehension between Argentina and Japan,
as a consequence of their knowledge of Japanese languag
and oustoms.
Ootober 12, 1943 (Tokyo 11th): A South Pac1fio base announces that at
6 p.m. on October 10, five Japanese Army planes fieroely
bombed the enemy base at Finschhafen, and despite heavy
flak, set big fires to enemy anti-aircraft emplacements and
ammunition dumps, and sank 3 large type enemy ships and set
fire to 2 others. One Japanese plane has not yet returned
from this fight.

October 13, 1943: uitorial on the spirituel bond rather than the
material bond which binds Japan and Argentina, as follows:
The Voice of "Diario Nan-A"
The points mentioned in the editorial published on
October l2 form the fundamental spirit of this paper, and
in accordance with them, we will write on subjects as cir-
cumstances require.
Since the outbreak of the present war, commercial in-
terchange between Argentina and Japan has been completely
suspended; no more comings and goings of tourists, and the
cultural contact of both nations has materially ceased to
exist. However, this contact is only superficial and ma-
terial. From the spiritual point of view we cannot but
recognize that the relationship of the two countries, far
from changing, has become stronger on account of the war.
As a consequence of the present situation, all correspon-
dence with our relations and friends in Japan has been out
off, but in spite of the great distance separating Argenti-
na from Japan and in spite of more than one and a half
years of no communioation, our love or our comprehonsion
of those in our motherland has not ohanged in the slightost;
on the contrary, it is increasing more and more. Sincerity
and sinoerity, friendship and friendship, in a word the
union of souls transcends space, time and changes of envi-
ronment.
We must not forget that friendly relations between na-
tions are not only maintained by economio-commercial contaet
but also by reciprocal sincerity, by the union of spiritual
strength. It often happens that relations formed solely
for material or utilitarian ends, either between individuals
or nations, turn to enmity when they are no longer profita-
ble. On the other hand, the friendly conneotion of the
spirit is lasting and cannot be destroyed by a breakdown of
interests.

=== Page 64 === (confidence 0.97)
一8一
The fact that for 46 years there has been between
Argentina and Japan, so far apart geographioally, such
excellent relations, which we believe will be permanent,
is evident proof that this relationship does not emanate
from material interests but from a solid spiritual bond.

U.S. Office of War Information, New Delhi.
DOMEI PRESS SERVIOE FOR THE PACIFIO ZONE
( Selected Items for your information Supplementing Cables )
13th September, 1943.
Manchoukuo -- Heavy Industries.
Hsinking, 13th: A recent survey oonduceed by economic experts
revealed that Manohoukuo's heavy industries, which are making valuable
contributions towards the prosecution of the Greater Fast Asian war
on the industrial front, have made great progress during the past six
years under the first and second Five-Year Plans for industrial develop-
ment. The end of the first Five-Year Programme in Maroh last year showed
an increase of 154% in the production of steel ingots over the beginning
of the programme, while the production of structural steel inoreased by
264, lead by 1200%, and zinc by 400k. At the end of Maroh this year,
marking the close of the first year under the second Five-Year Programme
further inoreases in producticn were recorded, pig iron 1noreasing by 1%
over the year before, steel ingots by 50x, struotural steel, 1l4, copper
300% and zinam over 200%. The Pen Hihu Iron and Coll1ery Company, which
completed the construotion of two new blast furnaces during the past two
years, now boasts of a record output of pig iron with very lor percentage
of phosphorus contents.
The Showa Steel Works at Anshan are also steadily increasing the steel
output through the ore process and will add another furnace whioh is
soheduled to be completed at the end of this year. Meanwhile, the pro-

Philippines -- Reconstruction.
Davao, 17th: One of the most revealing chapters in the history of
reconstruction of the new Philippines is the emergence of Mindanao as an
integral part of the independent nation to-be. In little more than a
year and a half since the outbreak of the war of Greater East Asia, the
Muslim populatin of Mindanao, which boasts of a long history of bloody
resistance against the U.S., is now oo-operating whole-heartedly with the
Filipinos in other regions of the Arohipelago in maintaining peace and
order as well as attaining eoonomic self-suffioienoy. It is recalled
that even upeo the out break of the war, Muslims resisted all efforts of
the Amerioan troops and constabulary men to accept U.S. sovereignty. That
the Muslims are at present co-operating with the authorities was shown
recently at a conference of Mayors and Governors held in Davao at which
the conferees pledged their unreserved co-operation with the Kalibapi
movement.
.. notable progress has been achieved towards the realisation of
economio self-sufficienoy. Hemp industry, whioh is one of the minstays
of Mindanao economically, has been given an added impetus while rice
production in .... and the copra industry of Zamboanga have also been
expanded greatly. Similarly, the planting of cinohona trees is also
undertaken on a large soale by the Government in their efforts to ensare
the future supply of quinine. Under the leadership of Sultan s Ramaim,
a member of the Preparatory Commission for Philippine Independenee, the
people of Mindanao, Muslims and Christians alike, are united as one man
to give a determined push to the reconstruction of Mindanao as an integral
part of reborn Philippines which will soon be achieving its independenee.

Hed China -- Wartime Profiteering.
Nanking, 17th: Aimed at wiping out wartime profiteering, the
National Defence Council of the National Government of Chiaa at yester-
day 's session, passed an amendment to paragraph 1 of Article 17 of the
revised special Wartime Criminal Law covering wartime profiteering. The
amendment will come into effect immediately. Under the new law, anyone
found guilty of speculative manupulation or cornering of daily neoessi
ties or military supplies aimed at profiteering or trading in securities
without a licenoe, or excessive profiteering in business transactions, or
manipulating the market causing unnecessary disturbance, is liable to
life imprisonment or lo,Ooo yuan fine or both. The meeting also passed
an amendment increasing the membership of the Standing Committee of the
National Economic Cojncil from eight to eleven. The new members named
are, Chen Chu Hui, Minister for Industry and Commeroe, Chen Chun Pu,
Minister for Reconstruction and Yu Chinm Ho, Director-General of Reson-
struotion Administration of the North China Politioal Affairs Couneil.

Manila, 19th: Farm tenants and farmers are no longer worried by
the problems of marketing their produce and are getting good prioes for
them, Benito M. Sakdalan, a veteran Filipino journalist disolosed in the
"Sunday Tribune" article entitled, "Small farmers enjoy a new life under
the new Philippines." He said that today dealers and traders approach
farmers to buy their products at the farmers' prices. He pointed outs
"The big problem that remains unsolved is the liquidation of the long
standing accounts of the small farmers with unsorupulous money-lenders
and farm tenants with their landlords, carried over from the American
regime. "
The present administration, however, plans to extend credit facili-
ties to farmers, whioh will gradually emanoipate them from the age-long
bondage of "gakdalan", the journalist asserted, adding: "Tt will relieve
many a small farmer of his lingering worry over his mortgaged land."
He said that the former high wall of frigid apathy and indifference so
often seen between the tenant and landlord during the American rule of
the Philippines has beeb wifed out, as a good feeling of neighbourliness
prevails. "The spirit of human justice and friendliness between the 
tenant and landlord is due to the Neighbourhoot Associations," sakdalan

A propos de la " Sixième bataille sérienne au large de Pougainvilla"
Commentant les résultets éclatents remportés par nos for-
ces aériennes de la Marine dens la sixième bataille aérienne au
large de l'ile de Bougainville, qui se déroule le 3 décembre, résul-
tats qui furent l'objet d'un communiqué du Grand Quartier Général
Impérial en date de hier et qui comportent 3 porte-avions, 1 cuiressé
ou grand croiseur, 1 autre croiseur coulés et 1 cuirassé et 1 gros
croiseur gravement endommegés, le commentateur politique déclara
que l'ennemi subit un coup sérieux depuis la contre-offensive dans
les Iles Solomons et le région des 'ilesGilbert. Les pertes navales
de l'ennemi s'eccumulent dans chaque tentative d'attaquer les posi-
tions jeponeises. Ses pertes sont particulièrement importantes en
porte-avions auxquels l'ennemi attribue une importance capit-le dans
ses opérations de contre-offensive.
A l: suite de son extension excessive, dans sa ligne de
ravitaillement, l'ennemi est forcé d'opérer dans le zone d'opérations
efficaces pour les Jeponeis et se trouve ainsi pris au piège.
Ainsi depuis le commencement des opérations contre l' le
de Mono, l'ennemi mobilise ses porte-avions dans le but de capturer
d'un seul coup Rabaul et en même temps de soutenir les operations de
déberquement dans l'fle de Bougainville.
Cependant, dens une série de six batailles aéro-navales
qui s déroulèrent au large de l'ile Bougainville, l'ennemi perdit
au total ll porte-avions. Ceci eut pour conséquence que la flotte
américaine du Pacifiqueusud-ouest se trouve complétement paralysée.
Ainsi, dens les opérations qui se déroulèrent dans les iles Gilbert
l'ennemi perdit ll porte-avions coulés par nos unités aériennes.
Ses pertes reprisentent pour l'ennemi un coup fatal car
une flotte dépourvue de porte-avions peut-être qualifiée de " cer-
cueil flottant " et perticulièrement une grande importance est
accordée aux porte-avions dans les opérations navales modernes.
Dans ces ciroonstances le haut-commandement des Etats Unis
fut obligé de continuer ses envois en porte-avions dans les régions

=== Page 118 === (confidence 0.97)
des iles Solomon et Gilbert. Pour effectuer toutes les opérations
de contre-offensive dans le zone de l'ile Solomon, la marine améri-
caine doit s'attendre aux attaques de la flotte jeponaise, et l'exécu-
tion de pareilles operetions de contre-offens vs est impossible sans
une puiasunte flotte de porte-avions. D'autre p rt, les forces impé
riale japonaises dans leurs atteques contre los foroes ennemies se
servent de beses invulnérebles. Par conséquent, les pertes ennemies
en bateaux et en effectifs nécessiteront l'augmentation des forces
dans cette zone d'opérations. Mais, un autre problèmo qui se pose
est que si les Etets Unis peuvent établir une iaportante flotte de
porte-avione dens un cour espece de temps, il ne sera pes possible
de former des pilotes et des marine pour ces porte-avions.
Voila lo grend problème qui se présente-è l'ennomi.

President Ichiji KURIBAYASHI and Secretary Teikichi
OKIMURA of the Japanese Association submit the following
business report for the 1943 Assooiation year (through
February, l944) for consideration of the members at the
annual meeting to be held on March 19, 1944 at the Japanese
Assooiation Hall, Patagones 840:
"According to the rules of the Association, the 1943-44
affairs of the Association and treasurer's report must be
submitted to the annual general meeting for consideration.
This past year has been full of diffioulties in the world,
but this Association has pursued its activities with modera-
tion. Although the end of the Association year found Japanese
Argentine foreign relations suddenly and unfortunately broken N
after 47 years of favorable progress, the Japanese in Argentin
were deeply distressed by this break. However, we, as a great
people, and as residents of this country, have done nothing
of whioh to be ashamed, and have never mixed ourselves in
politioal or theological movements. We have only sought to
advance the common welfare, and have believed in being open
and above-board in our aotions.
"In this new Assooiation year we shall devote all of
our strength to the development of our fellow countrymen
resident in Argentina, and the continuation of the enterpri-
ses whioh they have undertaken here. At the same, time, we
believe that this will contribute net a little to'the devel-
opment of Argentina. With the foregoing thought we submit
the following report of aohievements during the pAst year
in the form of direotor's meetings":
(There follows an account of numbers and dates of
direotor's meetings and sketohy reports of olub aptivitie
most of whioh have previously been translated: These of
S
possible interest, previously missed are as follows):
"On November 7th, 1943, the 14th servioe in cemme
tion of Japanese who died in Argentina was held, this time

over a dozen Japanese shop-keepers have been taken in

otc., work for several days, obtain advances of funds, then
disappear. Be careful of these tricksters. (END)
A number of Japanese business men have been taken in to
the extent of some Pesos 4,o00 by a man who goes under various
aliases such as Akio KAwASE or Shigeyoshi BISE or Henri Busta-
mante, who claims to be connected with a certain foreign firm,
temporarily out of work, but in position to be of assistance
to Japanese, and in need of funds which he will repay within
a few days. He then disappears. These types of oonfidence
men are takine advantage of the recent forced closing of the
Japanese Consulate. (DNA)

March 21st, 1944:
The Japanese Association of Argentina held its 19th
annual meeting on March 19th from 3 p.m. in the presence
of police officials. President Kuribayashi opened the
meeting with a few remarks. Mr. Kichihei HOSHI was elected
Chairman and Mr. Kiichiro SUZUKI, Vice chairman of the
meeting. Secretary Okimura and Treasurer Hattori read their
annual reports which were approved unanimously without ques-
tion. After the business meeting tea was served and the
ballots for the Association officers for l944 were opened.
As a result, in accordance with the wishes of the former
officers--although there were some who opposed the idea--if

February 1lth, 1944:
The report that a Japanese school in the suburbs had
been inspected by the police was completely false. In those
times all sorts of rumors are bound to be flying around, but
no matter what happens it is necessary that we keep calm. (m)
894.
Letters have been received from Mr. Hosokawa, and Master
Ryoioh1 FURUTA who were repatriated to Japan on the last ex-
change ship. (END)
The small Japanese community gets all exoited when it
thears that one of its number has been detained for investiga-
tion. We want to see you make up your minds to absolute
indifference to the entire 7,ooo of us being taken off to
the police station. (DNA)
3
13-21
While there have been a fair number of registrations
of those desiring to return to Japan to work for the country,

diffioulties in this country, you are also working for
Japan. (DNA)

Advice for the Times: In view of the increasing number
of arrests of Japanese since the break in relations, it is
aosired that everyone mutually take care on the following
points: Do not lose your head; don't talk with foreignors
about the situation; dcn't frequent conspiouous places; don't
hold meetings without obtaining permission; don't oall the
office or even the house of a person arrested by the author-
ities; don't hold long conversations on the phone; and don't
mix up your speech with Spanish. (DNA)

Editor Shintaku says that "it is really regrettablo
that Argontina broke relations with Japan. It has been
47 years since the treaty of commeroe and navigation was
signed between the two countries and 31 years since the
Japanese Legation and Consulate were established in Buonos
Aires.---At the time of the Russo-Japanese War the Argen-
tine sold two cruisers to the Japanese whioh figured
prominently in the decisive Battle of the Japan Sea.
Argentine Naval Attaché Garcia was on the flag-ship during
that battle and was a good friend of Admiral Togo. So the
spiritual ties between the two nations have always been
very close. However, international politios and diplomatio
relations are so complioated that they could not be adjusted
to simple friendly and spiritual relationships. The times
were simply not favorable to Argentine-Japanese relations."
(DNA)

the funotions of the Association shall be suspended tempo-
rarily". The proposal was finally approved by majority
vote as being unavoidable under present-day conditions with
relations broken between Japan and Argentina.

The Diario Nan-A began last December collecting funds
to present to the Argentine Army authorities for Air-Raid
Defense, as a measure of appreciation for the maintenance
of neutrality by Argentina. The collection of funds for
this purpose was temporarily suspended when a collection
was started for San Juan earthquake relief on January 17th.
Then came the break in Japanese-Argentine relations, and
the Diario Nan-A announoed that it had Pesos 11,S0s on hand
in the Air Raid Defense Fund whioh it was depesiting in the
bank and would make up a list of contributors to present
with the funds to the Argentine Army authorities. Subse-
quently there was considerable argument in the Japanese
community as to whether or not those funds sheuld be pre-
sented to the Argentine authorities inasmuch as they had
broken relations. It was finally decided to present them
in the name of Editor Shintaku or the Diario Nan-A. As a
result, the Argentine Army authorities annoumeed in all
Spanish language newspapers on April l2th that "Editer
Takaiohi SHINTAkU of the Diario Man-A deposited in the Ar-
gentine Central Bank on Maroh 21, Pesos 12,23l colleeted

from the Japanese community for the Air Raid Defense Fund,
and the War Ministry acknowledges receipt of these funds".
(EAD) (Note: This was reported to be part of an attempt
to get the Argentine Government to reconsider its break

June 18th, 1944:
Mr. Seizo IKEDA died on June 17th at Rawson Hospital
of paralysis after a long illness during the latter part
of which he was taken care of by Mr. Kojiro TANAkA of
Tanaka Restaurant. Mr. Ikeda is to be remembered as having
founded the first Japanese newspaper to appear in Buenos
Aires, the first issue being a new year number published
on January 7, 1922. The paper was mimeographed at first
and was oalled the A-koku Jiho (Argentine News) and then
continued with movable type. However, the enterprise
ended after a short time and in its plece appeared a
monthly magazine oalled Jitsugyo no Arg (Arg biz). This
in turn did not last long, but the present El Argentin
Djijo was an outgrowth of the start made by Mr. Ikeda.
(END)
The Aroma and Futaba Poetry Societies will hold a
joint meeting this afternoon at the José C. Paz Shippers
Association.(END)

spirit, with a Japanese loyalty that does not contradiot
Spanish loyalty and the spirit of San Martin. This despite
the fact that Japan sustains the prinoiple. of jus sanguinus
for citizenship while jus solis prevails in the Americas."

A-625, June 30, 4 p.m. Japanese Economis.
Rationalization of Labor and Materials - According to
a ndtice dated Tokyo, June 3, 1n the DEUTSCIE ZEITUNG M
OSTLAND, of June 4, 1944, the Japanese Armaments Ministry
has, for the purpose of the most rational utilization of
labor and materials, and following the German example, in-
troduced the denomination of "model establishments"
(Musterbetriebe). As ratio for the selection, an index
figure of l00 points has been set, in which the individual
factors have been allotted the following number of points:
utilization of production capacity 60, quality of production
15, quantitative performance 15, and financing 10.
Officially Re ulated Barter - In a notice dated Tokyo
June 6, 1n the DEUTSCIE ZEITUNG IN OSTLAND, of June 7, 1944,
1t is reported that the Japanese Trade Ministry has taken
the initiative in the governmental regulation of barter trade.
Control of barter will be assumed by a public company
which will have branches in every town, and which will
register all barter  oods, of which household utensils
textiles and (to a certain extent) production commodities
are mentioned. In this action, the Ministry is said to be
uided by the consideration that, in view of the closurof
a large number of small and medium-sized establishmentsq

number of rodaction means have become 1mmobilized

The goods thus collected will, 1f necessary, be altered
and repaired.
The barter transactions will be facilitated through
the fact that aypropriate rices will be fixed for the
goods by expert personnel drafted from trade circles.
Whoever delivers barter commodities shall receive a special
permit which, on a point system, entitles the owner to the
procurement of the required commodity by way of barter.
The Japanese barter action is expected to gain ime
portance through the participation of public administra-
lon offices which have been called upon to do their share
by preparing necessary 1tems for barter. Howover, it is
not expected thet these administrative offices will receive
in exchange other goods, except in special cases, since
their participation 1s meinly meant for the purpose of in
creasing the assortment of barter commodities.
